## 4. Data contract

### 4.1 Per-cell label table: `<sid>_celltype_labels.parquet`

One row per object in `<sid>_prepared.h5ad` (every segmented object). Column constants live in `src/merxen/annotation/schema.py` with `validate_label_table(df, species, h5ad_index=None)` (dtypes, categories, `cell_id` uniqueness, exact equality of `in_table` ids with the clustered H5AD index; R-eng). Categoricals as `category`, floats as float32.

**Identity and QC:** `cell_id` (str; equals prepared / clustered `obs_names` and the zarr table id); `instance_id` (int64, from `uns.spatialdata_attrs.instance_key`); `pair_id`, `sample_id`, `platform`, `segmentation`, `species`, `anatomical_region`, `panel_hash` (category); `total_counts`, `n_genes` (int32, after control removal); `genes_per_count`; `in_table` (`total_counts ≥ min_counts`); `depth_bin` (largest grid value ≤ the cell's counts; grid per bundle, §3.7); `resolvability_extrapolated` (bool: deeper than the deepest grid bin with ≥ 50 test cells of its class, D_max, §8.3); `n_missing_panel_genes` (per sample, repeated).

**Per-level columns.** Human levels `lineage`, `broad`, `nt`, `supercluster`, `seaad_subclass` (+ report-only `cluster`, OD-E4); mouse `broad`, `class`, `nt`, `subclass` (+ report-only `supertype`).

| Column | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ct_<L>_name` | category | Assigned label at L; null if not applicable or not attempted |
| `ct_<L>_raw` | float32 | Raw engine probability; coarse levels sum the bp of the assigned node and its same-parent runner-ups (E2 definition) |
| `ct_<L>_conf` | float32 | v1 = raw (`calibration="none"`); v1.1 simulation-calibrated (`calibration="simulation_ho"`) |
| `ct_<L>_corr` | float32 | MMC `avg_correlation` at L |
| `ct_<L>_runner_up`, `ct_<L>_margin` | category, float32 | Best alternative and raw margin |
| `ct_<L>_status` | category | §4.2 |
| `ct_<L>_validated` | bool | (rev3) The confident label lies in its panel family's validated region: `real_data` families up to `validated_max_level`; `simulation` families where (L, class) is validated and the cell's depth ≥ that row's `validated_min_depth` (§8.2, §4.7); false for provisional panels and for non-confident cells |

**Final and consensus columns.**

| Column | Type | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `ct_final_level`, `ct_final_name` | category | Deepest `confident` level up to `max_leaf_level`: human `none` < `lineage` < `broad` < `nt` < `supercluster`; mouse `none` < `broad` < `class` < `nt` < `subclass`; name `Mixed/Unknown` for `none`. Report-only fine levels never enter it |
| `ct_consensus_tier` | int8 | Human: methods agreeing at the 7-class level (2 = WHB and SEA agree; 1 = one informative; 0 = confident disagreement; up to 3 with LL in v1.1). Agreement, not accuracy (R-sci). Mouse: 1 |
| `ct_branch` | category | Human: `Neurons/Excitatory`, `Neurons/Inhibitory`, `Neurons/unresolved`, `Astrocytes`, `Oligodendrocytes`, `Oligodendrocyte precursors`, `Oligodendrocyte lineage/unresolved`, `Microglia`, `Vascular cells`, `Fibroblasts`, `Mixed/Unknown`. Mouse: WMB class or `Mixed/Unknown` |
| `ct_leaf` | category | Confident WHB supercluster (human) / WMB subclass (mouse), else `unresolved` |
| `ct_mender_state` | category | Fixed-level state for cross-dataset MENDER: human `ct_branch`, mouse class; never null |
| `soft_broad_<token>` / `soft_class_<token>` | float32 | Per-cell probability mass over broad classes (§5.5; residual in `soft_broad_unallocated`) / over the 34 WMB classes (mouse). Parquet only |

**Raw engine columns (parquet only):** `mmc_whb_{supercluster,cluster}_{label,name,bp,agg,corr}`, `mmc_whb_supercluster_runner_up_{1..5}_{name,bp}`; `mmc_whb_setc_supercluster_{name,bp}` or, for per-platform pairs, `mmc_whb_xpanel_supercluster_{name,bp}` (§8.5); `mmc_seaad_{class,subclass,supertype}_{name,bp,corr}`; mouse `mmc_wmb_{class,subclass,supertype,cluster}_{label,name,bp,agg,corr}`, `mmc_wmb_unpruned_{class,subclass}_{name,bp}`, `region_pruned_changed`, `inferred_region`; v1.1 `ll_{lineage,broad,supercluster}_{name,post}`, `ll_mix_fraction`, `ll_mix_partner`.

### 4.2 Status vocabulary (`CellStatus` StrEnum)

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `confident` | Passed the resolvability, threshold, floor, gate and agreement rules at this level |
| `low_confidence` | Raw or calibrated probability below the panel's threshold |
| `below_floor` | Counts below the class × platform × panel × level floor |
| `not_resolvable` | (rev2) The panel's resolvability table does not support this level for the parent class at the cell's depth bin, or the level is a report-only fine level that is not enabled (§8.3) |
| `method_disagree` | The second method disagrees (below 60 counts), or both are confident and disagree |
| `single_method` | Below 60 counts and the second method unavailable (§5.3) |
| `implausible` | Sink or implausible primary node (human: vocab `sink` or `region_plausible=false`; mouse: region-dropped without a confident re-map); falls back to the parent level |
| `parent_unresolved` | Parent level not confident |
| `not_attempted_gate` | Dataset gate level `broad_only` or `failed`, including a `broad_only` or `refused` panel (a gate warning alone never sets it) |
| `not_applicable` | e.g. `nt` for non-neurons |
| `low_counts` | `total_counts < min_counts`; not mapped |

### 4.3 Flag columns (bool, with continuous companions; report-only unless stated)

| Flag | Rule | Use |
|---|---|---|
| `flag_low_counts` | `total_counts < min_counts` | Excluded from tables (§4.4) |
| `flag_below_floor`, `flag_method_disagree`, `flag_implausible` | Mirror the statuses at the deepest attempted level | Status logic |
| `flag_cop_suppressed` | WHB called COP but the COP rule failed (§5.2) | Report |
| `contamination_score`, `neg_counts`, `flag_contaminated` | Score = counts on the assigned broad class's negative genes (§5.6) / total counts; flag = dataset-empirical beta-binomial test at α 0.01 with ≥ 3 negative counts; if the realised rate for a (class, platform) is > 15%, `flag_contaminated_informative=false` and the flag is null for that stratum | Report; DE covariate |
| `expected_genes_q95`, `flag_diffuse_profile` | `n_genes` above the q95 of distinct genes simulated at the cell's counts from the class's `profiles.parquet` multinomial; null if the realised rate is > 30% | Report |
| `ood_z`, `flag_ood` | Robust z (median/MAD) of `avg_correlation` within class × platform × depth bin, < −3 [L] | Report |
| `flag_region_incoherent`, `region_coherence` | Mouse F1 (E7 §3) | Report |
| `microglia_stat`, `microglia_weight`, `flag_microglial_spillover` | Mouse E3 specific-gene test with the panel's derived gene set (§8.6): statistic ≥ 10 and weight ≥ 0.05; null with a reason if < 3 specific genes; human off (OD-C5) | Spill-over prevalence; **never a microglia count** |
| `flag_astro_lowcount` | Mouse Astro-Epen call with < 100 counts (E3 residual sink) | Report; excluded in the "strict" Astro-Epen view |
| `exclude_hard` | `low_counts` or `implausible` at every level, or gate `failed` (incl. refused panel or failed registration) | Excluded from any composition or DE |
| `discovery_caution` | Any of contaminated, diffuse, ood, method_disagree, microglial_spillover, astro_lowcount | Keep these cells, add `contamination_score` as a DE covariate, run a with/without sensitivity (R-sci); excluded from the v1.1 split core only if validated (M11) |

Removed from the draft: the per-cell `flag_seaad_disease_supertype` (fires on 7–27% of in-silico neurotypical microglia, E1 §6(3); population level only, §9 item 11) and `exclude_from_discovery` (replaced by `exclude_hard` + `discovery_caution`).

### 4.4 Cell-set policy

- **Unchanged v1 contract:** the clustered H5AD and zarr table hold exactly the objects with `total_counts ≥ min_counts` (10) after control removal. `select_table_cells` is shared with legacy `run_scanpy_clustering` (unit test: identical sets, so MENDER set equality holds); `hard_min_counts` = `min_counts`. On current panels the control registry removes exactly what `remove_control_features` removes (test); extra control types on new panels (e.g. intergenic, §8.4) are removed in MAP only, so table membership still follows the shared helper [L; checked in M3b].
- **The parquet covers all segmented objects,** and the report gives both denominators (e.g. P1212_M confident broad coverage 0.442 of table cells vs 0.153 of segmented objects; `exp/E2/out/final_coverage_and_gate.csv`, LL rule). Low-count objects in the table: OD-D9 (adopted: no).

### 4.5 Clustered H5AD `obs`, the zarr table, and legacy columns (map_first)

FINALIZE copies `obs` into the zarr table unchanged. From the parquet (table cells only): all `ct_*` except `soft_*`; flags and companions; `exclude_hard`, `discovery_caution`, `depth_bin`. `mmc_*` and `ll_*` stay in the parquet.

| Column | map_first content |
|---|---|
| `broad_class` | `vocab.broad_class_for_map_first(ct_final)`: `ct_broad_name` if confident; else `Oligodendrocyte lineage` if that lineage is confident; else `Neurons` if lineage is confident; else `Mixed/Unknown`. Sinks → `Mixed/Unknown`. `Ependymal` (and mouse `Astrocytes/Ependymal`) follows the species vocab; in human cortex it is `region_plausible=false` → `Mixed/Unknown`. The legacy `collapse_atlas_label_to_broad_class` stays **byte-identical** (MECR `mecr.py:1169` and the legacy path use it; R-eng) |
| `broad_atlas_label` | `ct_supercluster_name` (human) / `ct_class_name` (mouse) when confident, else `broad_class` |
| `broad_annotation_score`, `_score_margin` | `ct_broad_conf` / `ct_broad_margin` (deprecated aliases) |
| `broad_annotation_n_markers` | Panel root markers in the primary lookup (constant; deprecated) |
| `leiden_broad`, `leiden` | Whole-section QC Leiden at 0.5 (CPU igraph, seed 0); not used for routing |
| `neuron_split_label` | `Excitatory` / `Inhibitory` / `Other` / `unresolved` / `not_neuron` from the taxonomy NT vocab; the viewer's case-insensitive match keeps working |
| `subcluster_label` | = `ct_leaf` |
| `subcluster_status` (new) | `mapped_supercluster`, `mapped_subclass`, `unresolved`, `not_resolvable_panel`, `dataset_broad_only`, `unassigned`; v1.1 adds `denovo_split`, `denovo_unsplit`, `denovo_unvalidated_depth` |
| `hierarchical_cluster` | `f"{ct_branch}:{ct_leaf}"`: categorical, never null, globally unique; e.g. `Neurons/Excitatory:Upper-layer intratelencephalic`, `Mixed/Unknown:unresolved`, mouse `01 IT-ET Glut:007 L2/3 IT CTX Glut`. Broad-only datasets have all leaves `unresolved`. With `leaf_source="denovo"` (v1.1): `<branch>:<supercluster>:<k>` |
| `denovo_subcluster` (v1.1) | Exploratory, within-dataset only |

`obsm` `X_pca` / `X_umap` (QC embedding), `spatial` and `layers["counts"]` are kept; `obsp` from the QC graph.

### 4.6 `uns` provenance (safe for h5ad and zarr)

- **Scalars and JSON strings only:** keys never contain `/` (h5ad nests silently; zarr raises `ValueError`) — per-branch keys use `_safe_token(branch)`; no lists of dicts (h5ad `TypeError`; R-eng M2, anndata 0.12).
- **`uns["merxen_annotation_json"]`** = JSON string of `AnnotationProvenance`, also written to `<sid>_annotation_manifest.json`: `schema_version`, `mode`, `species`, `anatomical_region`; **panel** (`panel_hash`, `panel_family`, `panel_mode`, `panel_trust`, `validation_basis` (with the `validated_panel_levels.csv` sha256 and the validated share per level), real-data QC outcomes and downgrades (§8.8), gene-ID resolution summary by source, unmapped count, controls removed by type, `panel_report_sha256`); references (id, role, taxonomy, levels, `n_leaves`, collapsed parents, `nodes_dropped`, `drop_level`, `bundle_path`, `build_hash`, source identities and sha256, `n_query_genes_used`); markers (lookup sha256, `n_per_utility`, `n_panel_genes`, markers per parent min / median, prefilter); **resolvability** (recipe and version, sha256, emitted levels per class and depth bin, D_max and extrapolated share per class, reweighting applied, `resolvability_inherited`, resolvable share per level); engine (ctm version and commit, bootstrap factor / iterations, seed, `n_processors`, wall time); thresholds with `threshold_source`, floors sha256 with `floor_source`, calibration kind; flag thresholds, flag gene sets and realised rates per class × platform; dataset gate (A, coverages, level `full` / `broad_only` / `failed`, warning flag and reasons); mouse gate signals and verdict, `section_regions`, `n_assigned_tiles`, drop-list sha256 and size, `n_remapped`, the M6b rule variant; confident fraction per level of table cells and of segmented objects.
- **`uns["merxen_hierarchical_clustering"]`** (existing) gains flat scalars `mode`, `branch_level`, `leaf_source`, `engine`, `engine_n_iterations`, `engine_seed` and `branch_manifest_json` (JSON keyed by safe tokens).

### 4.7 Vocabulary and packaged tables (`src/merxen/annotation/vocab.py`; `src/merxen/assets/annotation/*`)

- `whb_supercluster_vocab.csv` (31 rows): `label, name, broad_class, lineage, nt, sink, region_plausible_frontal_cortex`. `nt` = majority WHB NT annotation (`CCN202210140_NEUR`, `clustering_squidpy.py:61`); `sink` = Splatter, Miscellaneous; `region_plausible_frontal_cortex` false for the 16 superclusters pruned in E1 (ii); the column is keyed by `anatomical_region`, so a new region adds a column, not code (OD-C7, §8.9).
- `seaad_mr_subclass_vocab.csv` (29 rows): `subclass, broad_class, lineage, nt`, with supertype overrides for "VLMC & Perivascular" (VLMC → Fibroblasts; Pericyte / SMC → Vascular cells). `whb_supercluster_majority` is **not committed**: derived from expression via `exp/E1/seaad_to_whb_crosswalk.csv` and built in PREP into `bundle.json` (R-fea, R3).
- `wmb_class_vocab.csv` (34 rows): `class, broad_class, nt, never_drop`.
- `floors_human.csv`, `floors_mouse.csv` (§5.4), keyed by (level, class, platform, panel family).
- `state_genes_human.csv`: curated disease / state genes excluded from negative lists, each with a rationale; seed CTSD, SERPINA3, SOX2, CDKN1A, APOE, GFAP, CD44, C3, HSPA1A, HSP90AA1 [L; reviewed in M4].
- **(rev2)** `validated_panels.csv` (family, `panel_hash`, species, platform(s), gene count, `validated_max_level`, `validation_basis` (`real_data` | `simulation`; rev3), evidence path, date, approving PR; seeded with human set a — the 296-gene evidence hash and the post-M0e hash (297 if H2AX resolves) — with its curated family set c (E5's 32 exclusions; M2 review), ag7 500, VZG2 815, all `real_data`) and, for `simulation` rows only, the companion `validated_panel_levels.csv` (rev3 check; family, `panel_hash`, level, class, `in_class_set` (C_P, §14), status `validated` | `failed:<NP id>` | `not_evaluable`, `validated_min_depth`, `tested_max_depth` (D_P; deeper bins inherited and marked extrapolated), evidence path), which RESOLVE applies per (level, class); `control_features.yaml` (control registry, §8.4); `gene_id_overrides_{human,mouse}.csv` (curated rows with reasons; seeded with the four M0e genes).
- `NOTICE`: provenance of every row (Allen taxonomy release, CCN ids) and licences (CC BY-NC 4.0 data; Allen Institute Software License for ctm).
- **Generator** `scripts/annotation/build_vocab_tables.py` (Allen taxonomy CSVs + `overrides.yaml`; output committed and reviewed). Species vocabularies stay separate (human 7 classes + `Oligodendrocyte lineage`; mouse 6), both with `UNASSIGNED_LABEL = "Mixed/Unknown"`. `vocab.py`, `schema.py` and `annotation/config.py` import only numpy, pandas and pydantic (GPU-env safe).

### 4.8 SpatialData table keys

- New `src/merxen/table_keys.py` (M1): `ANALYSIS_LAYER_KEYS` (mirrors `analysisLayerKeys`, `main.nf:372-406`) and `clustered_table_key(source_table_key, segmentation, suffix="")`, with the same three return paths as `main.nf` (reseg / `table_MOSAIK_proseg`, original_seg / `table_original`, default).
- **Four copies today** must all get the suffix: `main.nf:408-415` (which also feeds MENDER and downstream); `clustering_squidpy._clustered_spatialdata_table_key` (`:1315-1326`); `cortical_depth/pipeline.py:411-422`; the external viewer (`../napari-spatialomics-viewer`, `tests/test_overlays.py`). `feature/gaston` adds a fifth (§2.4). The Python copies import `table_keys`; the `main.nf` function takes the suffix (H2); `tests/test_workflows/test_table_keys_sync.py` asserts equality with the Python mapping on all three return paths with and without a suffix, and that no other `main.nf` function builds a `_clustering_squidpy` key. The viewer gets an issue in its repo.
- **Suffix before the flip (OD-A3, decided):** a map_first run of a species that has not flipped writes `<src>_clustering_squidpy_mapfirst`, so the legacy table is never overwritten; legacy runs always write the unsuffixed key; downstream stages read the key through `table_key_suffix`; after the flip the suffix is `""`.
- **Viewer:** no `proseg_hybrid` source (wiring map §c; issue in that repo); manual checks use reseg or original_seg.

### 4.9 MENDER state policy

- Unassigned cells (`Mixed/Unknown`, `*/unresolved`) are 28–61% of table cells (P7513_M .28, P7513_X .46, P1212_M .55, P1212_X .61; `review_robustness/floor_bias_coverage.csv`); as a state they would make niches track depth and quality (R-rob).
- `MenderConfig.unassigned_state_policy`: `"state"` (current; legacy default) or `"exclude_from_features"` (unassigned cells stay spatial nodes but add no state to neighbourhood features; map_first default, OD-B4 adopted). Implemented in `src/merxen/analysis/mender.py` (around `:118-148`).
- Gate level, warning flag and panel trust state go into the MENDER and cortical-depth manifests; cross-dataset or cross-platform MENDER uses `ct_mender_state`; datasets from `provisional` panels are listed separately in cohort outputs.

## 5. Problem 2: reference-based annotation

### 5.1 Engines and references

- **Human** (OD-B2, decided): `whb_frontal_supc_clus` (primary; naming for all classes in v1); `seaad_mr_panel` (required second vote, concordance, disease supertypes); `whb_frontal_supc_clus` on set c or the intersection panel (sensitivity, §8.5); `whb_frontal_supc_clus_ho` (resolvability, §8.3); LL (`ll_whb_ctx_profiles`) in v1.1 if M3 shows value.
- **Mouse:** `wmb_panel` (Allen means + panel lookup), `wmb_selfmap_testset` (resolvability), `wmb_region_share`. Legacy MAPMYCELLS is not used by the new path.

**Why WHB-frontal.** You asked to keep Allen WHB, and it is best or tied on every real-data proxy: JSD .132 / .143 vs .149 / .242 for whole WHB pruned; sinks 0.3–0.6% vs 1.5–4.0%; the marker referee sides with (iii) in 52–63% of disputes. The case for SEA-AD as primary is weak at broad level (R-sci): in silico .963 (leaky) vs .967; ECE .061 vs .020; JSD .132 vs .146 without CIs; referee tie. Real microglia fit SEA-AD better in 93–99% of cells (E1 §6; [L], SEA-AD Immune includes PVM and monocytes). M3 reports the JSD difference with a bootstrap CI for information; changing the naming reference would need a new decision.

### 5.2 Human rules v1 (`consensus.resolve_human`, raw thresholds)

Rules apply to table cells; every level is also subject to the panel's resolvability (`not_resolvable` where the table does not support it, §8.3). Thresholds are the validated defaults, which a panel without real-data validation may raise (D-G3). "SEA agrees" = SEA's 7-class label (via the vocab) equals WHB's; "SEA confidently disagrees" = SEA broad raw ≥ 0.68 on a different class.

1. **Lineage** (Neurons / Oligodendrocyte lineage / Astrocytes / Microglia / Vascular / Fibroblasts) is `confident` if WHB lineage-aggregated raw ≥ 0.73, the node is not a sink or implausible, counts ≥ the hard floor, and either counts ≥ 60 with SEA not confidently disagreeing or counts < 60 with SEA agreeing at lineage; else `low_confidence`, `method_disagree`, `single_method` or `implausible`. An implausible node keeps its lineage when SEA agrees at lineage.
2. **Broad** requires lineage `confident`, WHB broad raw ≥ 0.73, counts ≥ `floor[broad][class][platform, panel]` and the same SEA rule at the 7-class level. **COP rule:** a WHB "Committed oligodendrocyte precursor" call gets broad "Oligodendrocyte precursors" if the COP supercluster rule passes (≥ 120 counts and supercluster raw ≥ 0.69) **or** SEA-AD confidently calls OPC; otherwise lineage "Oligodendrocyte lineage" with `flag_cop_suppressed`.
3. **NT (neurons):** aggregated Exc/Inh raw ≥ 0.73 and counts ≥ the floor (Inh: MERSCOPE 10, Xenium 30); no dedicated NT calibration [L].
4. **Supercluster:** broad `confident`, gate level `full` (a warning flag does not block), WHB supercluster raw ≥ 0.69, counts ≥ `floor[supercluster][class][platform, panel]`, and resolvable for the broad class at the depth bin; COP only at ≥ 120 counts.
5. **SEA-AD subclass** (secondary name, never in `ct_final`): gate level `full`, counts ≥ the supercluster floor of its broad class, raw ≥ 0.55 (< 60 counts) or ≥ 0.45 (≥ 60), SEA broad agrees with `ct_broad`; `calibrated=false` in every figure.
6. **WHB cluster:** never a leaf or in `ct_final`; reported as `ct_cluster_*` only with `allow_fine_levels` and a resolvability pass (OD-E4, decided 2026-09-26). On the validated set-a panel resolvability is expected to deny it (calibrated coverage ≤ 5.7%, E2).
7. **Final label** = deepest `confident` level along lineage → broad → nt → supercluster. 8. **Tier** is computed independently and reported as agreement, not accuracy.

**Coverage cost of the below-60 SEA rule: unmeasured [L].** M3 measures it. **Trigger for promoting LL (OD-B13):** if the rule removes > 8 points of confident broad coverage on any dataset vs the E2 LL rule, the LL part of M10 moves before M8 (+4–6 days on the human path), decided in the M3 PR. The separate LL value test (M3 item 6) only decides whether LL joins v1.1 (OD-B8).

### 5.3 Degraded modes (unit-tested truth table)

| Methods available | Rule below 60 counts | Status if the second method is missing | Max tier |
|---|---|---|---|
| WHB + SEA (v1 default) | SEA must agree | – | 2 |
| WHB + SEA + LL (v1.1) | SEA **or** LL agrees (LL "OtherNeuron" counts as Neurons) | – | 3 |
| WHB + LL | LL must agree | – | 2 |
| WHB only (SEA failed, disabled, or `refused` for this panel) | Not confident | `single_method` (override `annotation_allow_single_method=true`, recorded) | 1 |
| SEA only (WHB failed or refused) | Statuses only: the primary is required | `not_attempted_gate` | – |

SEA thresholds are read from the SEA bundle (v1.1 derives them from its calibration table, so a new release updates them; R-rob). Each reference uses the panel genes it contains — WHB frontal 59,357, SEA-AD 36,601, WMB 32,285 genes, all Ensembl [H] — and the per-reference count is recorded (`n_query_genes_used`).

### 5.4 Floors and dataset gate

**Floor algorithm** (`thresholds.derive_floors`; runs on the resolvability simulation in v1 and on the calibration simulation in v1.1): per (level, class, platform, panel family), the smallest grid depth with precision ≥ target (broad 0.90, supercluster 0.85; local isotonic rule, §8.3) and ≥ 50 test cells at that depth; with n < 50 inherit the other platform's floor where it has n ≥ 50, else the hard floor (`floor_source=default_low_n`). **Panels validated on real data** use the packaged real-derived floors below (`floor_source=real_e2`; simulated floors reported beside them). **Any other panel or platform** (Xenium 5K, custom Xenium v1, custom MERSCOPE, mouse on Xenium) gets max(maximum over known platforms and validated panels, the panel's simulated floor) per class and level, with `floor_source=unknown_panel` and a warning (R-rob; D-G3); families validated by simulation (OD-E1) keep the same max rule with `floor_source=simulation_validated` and no warning (§8.2).

**v1 values for validated panels** (`floors_human.csv`: the precision-only column of `review_robustness/floors_rederived.csv`, from E2 real self-thinning, `exp/E2/floors.py`, with the n ≥ 50 inheritance):

| Level | Class | MERSCOPE | Xenium | Note |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| broad | Astro, Exc, OPC, Oligo, Vascular | 10 | 10 | Xenium Oligo inherits MERSCOPE (Xenium n = 5) |
| broad | Immune | 10 | 15 | |
| broad | Inh | 10 | 30 | Xenium n = 1,332 |
| broad | OtherNeuron | 60 | 60 | MERSCOPE (n = 15) inherits Xenium |
| supercluster | Astro, Exc, Oligo | 10 | 10 | Xenium Oligo inherited |
| supercluster | Immune | 10 | 15 | |
| supercluster | Inh | 30 | 10 | |
| supercluster | Vascular / Fibroblast | 10 | 30 | |
| supercluster | OPC / COP | 120 | 120 | |
| supercluster | OtherNeuron | 60 | 60 | |

These rest on self-thinned real cells with ≥ 200 counts (self-referential per R-sci), so [M]; v1.1 re-derives them from HO simulation with binomial CIs. Mouse v1 (MERSCOPE): class 20 and subclass 50 for every class [L] plus `flag_astro_lowcount` (< 100), re-evaluated in M6; mouse on other panels or Xenium follows the unknown-panel rule, with a subclass floor of at least 60 while provisional (§8.2).

**Dataset gate (pre-registered; `thresholds.dataset_gate`):** the verdict is a **level** — `full`, `broad_only` or `failed` — plus a separate **warning flag** with its reasons. A = fraction of table cells with ≥ 30 counts; level `broad_only` if A < 0.30; `failed` if confident broad coverage of table cells < 0.25; warning if confident broad coverage of segmented objects < 0.15. The trust state can force `broad_only` or `failed` (§8.2); a `provisional` panel sets the warning flag and banner but never lowers the level, so rules 4–5 still run on it under resolvability and the provisional margins. A family validated by simulation sets the warning flag only when > 10% of a dataset's confident labels at an emitted level fall outside its validated region (`warn_unvalidated_share`, §8.2).
- Expected: P1212_M (A .234) and P5011_M (A .127) `broad_only`; P5011_M also warns (.093 of segmented objects); the rest `full`.
- A is a depth-support proxy and panel-dependent; it passes trivially on deep data, and for 5K panels the resolvability table decides what the depth supports (5K cells are not necessarily deep: one benchmark reports about 60% of 5K-panel cells failing low-count QC; `research/lowcount_segmentation_robustness.md:208`). Mouse has its own gate (§7.6).
- **Why not C:** predicted from simulation it no longer separates the broad-only datasets (−.099 / −.112 vs −.088 to −.103; `review_robustness/gate_C_sim.csv`); E2's separation used real pooled thinned rates that production lacks.
- **v1.1 gate (M10):** bundle-predicted supercluster coverage at target precision for the dataset's depth histogram and composition (v1 reports the uncalibrated version from the resolvability table), a self-thinning validity check where ≥ 500 cells have ≥ 200 counts (E2 verdict 2), and LL agreement D; replaces A only after re-validation on all 8 human datasets.
- Broad-only datasets: supercluster and SEA subclass `not_attempted_gate`; `subcluster_status = "dataset_broad_only"`; excluded from supercluster-level cross-platform statistics; broad-level statistics under OD-B6.

### 5.5 Composition estimator (all cross-platform and cross-dataset statistics)

- **Soft composition:** per table cell, the WHB bp of the assigned supercluster plus its runner-ups aggregated to broad through the vocab, residual → `unallocated`; composition = sum over cells; JSD on the renormalised 7-class vector with the unallocated share alongside.
- The reviewer's simpler version (assigned-class bp only) gave JSD .139 / .129 / .136 / .279 (P7513 / P1212 / P7113 / P5011), close to all-cell argmax (.131 / .119 / .113 / .248), on E2 `cells_native` (8-class scheme); E1 `real_jsd.csv` gives .132 / .143 (P7513 / P1212) under its scheme. The runner-up extension and production scheme are re-measured in M3 [M].
- **CI:** spatial block bootstrap over 500 µm tiles, 200 replicates. **Also reported:** whole section vs shared mask; depth-stratified (≥ 30-count cells); confident-only (expected higher, .236 / .285 / .327 / .455 under the draft floors). Per-platform pairs use the intersection run (§8.5). Mouse uses the same estimator over WMB classes.

### 5.6 Flags: design details

- **Negative genes** (`negative_genes.parquet`, PREP): per broad class, genes with `gt0/n_cells < 1%` in that class in both WHB-frontal and SEA-AD Multiregion (SEA supertypes mapped via the vocab), minus `state_genes_human.csv`. Mouse: WMB panel precompute only, minus a mouse state list (Apoe, Gfap, C4b, Serpina3n, H2-K1) [L]. For 5K panels the lists are much longer; the score is a fraction of counts and the dataset-empirical null absorbs the scale [L; M13].
- **Contamination null:** per (broad class, platform), a beta-binomial on the negative counts of that class's confident cells in the top depth quartile; flag tail p < 0.01 with ≥ 3 negative counts; realised rates recorded. It finds cells more contaminated than typical deep cells of their class in the same dataset, not an absolute FPR (the reference null fired on 17–75%, `review_robustness/contam_flag_rate_real.csv`; simulated neuron q95 is 0.000 at every depth, `contam_sim_q95.csv`).
- **Diffuse:** multinomial expectation from the class profile, 200 simulations per grid point, interpolated; replaces the draft's tercile rule, which flags a third of cells by construction (16–33%; `review_robustness/diffuse_flag_rate.csv`). Native cells carry 30–45% more genes than thinned ones (E2), so a high realised rate is expected and then marked uninformative.
- **Microglial spill-over (mouse):** the E3 `09b_specific_flag.py` test (TAU 10, fixed 10% ambient) on the panel's derived gene set (§8.6); runtime FPR check: flag rate among marker-astrocytes (Aqp4+ on current panels, the derived astrocyte set otherwise) ≤ 0.5%, else `flag_microglial_spillover_informative=false`; flag rate shown against depth (TAU validated only at 430–990 counts; R-rob).
- **Disease state:** SEA-AD "-SEAAD" supertypes summarised per class at population level against the in-silico baseline (24–56% of real vs 7–27% of in-silico microglia; E1 §6(3)); no per-cell flag.

### 5.7 v1.1 simulation calibration (M10; HO by default)

- **Moved into v1 (rev2):** the HO simulator itself (test cells, thinning to the grid, the fixed R1_contam_HO recipe, production mapping, `derive_floors` on simulation) and its isotonic per-cell threshold step with composition reweighting run in PREP / RESOLVE as the resolvability step (§8.3, M3b, M4); M10 builds on them.
- **HO by default:** `annotation_calibration_holdout_donor = "auto"` (the donor with the fewest frontal cells, H19.30.002, as in the pilot; §3.2). All 2,100 in-silico test neurons came from H19.30.002, which is inside the frontal precompute, so the non-HO recipe is leaky (R-sci). HO precompute and lookup: `exp/E2/ho/`.
- **M10 adds:** per-gene efficiency from **measured** per-platform factors (`research/xplat`, capped ±2 log2) plus LogNormal(0, 0.4) residual (instead of LogNormal(0, 0.8)); contamination from the **measured human own-fraction distribution per platform** (`exp/E3/10_ownfrac.py` method on P7513 / P1212, neighbour-type donors); truth = majority component; 30–50k cells; per-cell calibrated-probability output ("simulation-calibrated probability", per level, depth bin and engine, on the v1 isotonic tables and their composition reweighting); floors with binomial CIs; SEA thresholds from its table. Measured factors and own-fraction need a platform's real data, so a new platform keeps the fixed v1 recipe until its first datasets exist (OD-E2).
- **Validation (non-circular):** held-out-gene enrichment (§5.8); HO in-silico precision and coverage reweighted to each dataset. Real self-thinning is a QC diagnostic only (its truth is the same engines' full-depth call).
- **Cost:** panel extraction ~18 min (`research/insilico/01_extract.py`) + ~35 CPU-min mapping per engine at 50k cells (≤ 815 genes).

### 5.8 Held-out-gene enrichment (non-circular; `scripts/acceptance/heldout_genes.py`)

1. Per broad class choose 2–3 canonical markers present on the platform from an independent list (`assets/annotation/heldout_markers_{human,mouse}.csv`), e.g. human SLC17A7 / SATB2 (Exc), GAD1 / GAD2 (Inh), AQP4 / GJA1 (Astro), MOBP / PLP1 (Oligo), PDGFRA / VCAN (OPC), CSF1R / P2RY12 (Microglia), CLDN5 / FLT1 (Vascular), DCN / LUM (Fibroblast). Check panel membership per panel [L]; avoid SST, and GAD2 / P2RY12 on MERSCOPE (poorly detected; P7513 report F4). Classes with < 2 held-out markers on a panel are skipped and reported.
2. Remove them from the query **and** the lookup; re-map (WHB only; one extra MMC run).
3. Test the held-out markers' detection in the assigned class vs other classes: fold enrichment and AUROC per class and platform.
4. Mouse: the E3 VZG2 microglial test (35–40×) plus the same procedure at class level.

Runs in the acceptance scripts (M3, M8, M9; in M13 only as an optional downgrade-only check on in-house datasets of new families, §8.8), not in production.

### 5.9 What v1 does *not* do

- No per-cell AD glial states (the WHB primary is neurotypical; a 0.9-precision supercluster gate does not separate graded states). Instead: population-level disease-supertype fractions; SEA-AD glial supertypes as a report column (`calibrated=false`, OD-B3); continuous state scores in v2.
- No leaves finer than WHB supercluster / WMB subclass, even on 5K panels (OD-E4, decided 2026-09-26: finer levels only as report-only opt-in columns). No human region other than frontal cortex (OD-C7).
- The v1.1 de novo split can surface replicating reactive states (e.g. SERPINA3 / DNER astrocytes, AUROC .87–.92; SFRP2 / IGFBP5, .90–.98; E4 §B), as exploratory output only.

---

## 6. Problem 1: a hierarchy that is robust to data quality

### 6.1 Principles

- **Map first (D-H1).** Branch = `ct_branch`, leaf = `ct_leaf`; a cell's depth in the hierarchy is its deepest confident level. Poor data give fewer, coarser labels rather than wrong fine ones — per cell through resolvability, thresholds and floors; per dataset through the gate and the panel trust state.
- **Leaves are reference nodes the dataset can resolve** (D-H2; OD-B1, decided): the confident WHB supercluster / WMB subclass, comparable across platforms, donors, segmentations and panels (needed by the MENDER key and cross-platform analyses). The draft's de novo count-split merge inside broad + NT re-derived supercluster structure (P7513 Exc: L2/3 IT, L4/5 IT, Car3-like, deep; AMI vs SEA subclass .71 / .77) with dataset-local ids, contradicting "de novo only below the resolvable level" (R-rob, R-fea).
- **No fixed resolution decides anything:** 0.5 is used only for the QC Leiden (§6.3) and, in v1.1, to *propose* merge candidates.
- **One Leiden engine:** CPU `leidenalg`/igraph, `n_iterations=2`, recorded seed, for any partition reaching outputs (CPU vs GPU ARI 0.57–0.91 on the same graph; `code/clustering_map.md` §8).

### 6.2 `map_first.run_map_first_hierarchy` (v1)

1. Read the label parquet, align on `obs_names`, assert equality with `validate_label_table(..., h5ad_index=adata.obs_names)`.
2. Branches `ct_branch`, leaves `ct_leaf`; branches under 50 cells keep their leaves; mouse classes under 50 cells get leaf `unresolved`.
3. Fill the legacy columns (§4.5) and `subcluster_status`. 4. QC embedding and Leiden (§6.3).
5. Manifest (safe tokens, JSON strings) and plots: UMAP and spatial maps by `broad_class`, `ct_leaf`, `ct_final_level`; per-branch dotplots reusing `compute_group_gene_summary` / `plot_group_gene_dotplot` (`clustering_squidpy.py:1039-1261`).

The legacy 0.15 neuron-split round and fixed branch resolutions do not exist in map_first (they stay in legacy until M12).

### 6.3 Whole-section QC clustering and stability diagnostic

- Leiden 0.5 on the current representation, CPU igraph, seed 0, in `leiden_broad` / `leiden`, for UMAP and QC only. E6: within 0.03 ARI of the optimum against corrected SEA-AD subclass labels in 4/4 human sections (.961 / .948 / .971 / .910); ag7 also peaks at 0.5 (ARI .815). 5K panels use the top 2,000 HVGs [L].
- `clustering/stability.py`: O(T·N) contingency-table ARI over 5 subsamples of 80% (prototype `research/resolute_eval/fast_stability.py`, exact against O(N²)); reported, **never** used to choose a resolution (the "largest resolution with ARI ≥ 0.8" rule overshoots by 0–4 grid steps, E6).

### 6.4 v1.1 (M11, opt-in, default off; OD-B1): within-supercluster de novo split

- **Rule:** the E4 depth-matched count-split merge *inside confident superclusters* (human; mouse subclasses as QC only): candidate Leiden 0.5 on core cells; merge adjacent pairs below held-out balanced accuracy τ 0.90 (logistic regression on log-normalised X2, depth-matched within 10 log-depth bins, 20 for mouse, ≤ 3,000 cells per side); minimum child max(100 core cells, 1% of core); K = 1 → `denovo_unsplit`.
- **Core** = E4 definition (`total_counts ≥ max(20, q30)`, `n_genes ≥ 10`, no `exclude_hard`); excluding `discovery_caution` cells is a tested variant, not the default (E4 validated without exclusions; R-sci, R-rob). **Draws:** single draw, seed 0; "majority over 3 draws" is an untested variant evaluated in M11 (R-fea).
- **Regime diagnostic** per branch (merges performed, minimum rejected pair accuracy, core median depth vs the validated ~41–150 counts): `denovo_unvalidated_depth` when the core median is > 300 or no merge fired (at 866 counts τ never binds and K = the candidate K; E4 §F; R-rob). Deep data such as 5K panels therefore fall back to mapped leaves by a data-keyed rule, not by species.
- **Projection** of non-core cells by kNN majority (k 15, minimum vote 0.5), validated by thinning core cells to non-core depth (target ≥ 0.85 [L]). **Null check** (gamma-Poisson single population) is a sanity check only; contamination strata (the failure E4 saw, §B) are caught via `contamination_score` (a subcluster with mean score > 2× its parent's is flagged).
- **Cross-dataset use:** exploratory and within-dataset only; compare K only after thinning the deeper dataset to the shallower median.
- **Validation before any default:** held-out donors P7113 and P5011; reseg of P7513 / P1212; production branches; non-trivial passes (both platforms split) reported apart from "both unsplit".
- **Evidence, honestly stated:** E4 table A, τ 0.90 passes 4/6, three of them "both unsplit" (P1212 Oligo, P1212 Astro, P7513 Oligo); only P7513 Exc is non-trivial (4/5 clusters, replication 0.89, η² ≤ 0.06). τ 0.95 scores 6/6 while collapsing P7513 Exc to 2–3 clusters. P1212 MERSCOPE Exc is unsplit under every τ (the X2 half holds ~20 counts), although fixed 0.5 finds 3–5 replicable clusters there (replication .75).

### 6.5 Decision on RESOLUTE (not adopted)

1. **Maturity:** pre-release, 0 stars or forks, no tests, no declared dependencies, a PyPI name collision (`research/resolute.md` §0, §4). GPL-3.0 is *not* a reason: MerXen already depends on GPL `leidenalg` and `python-igraph` (`pyproject.toml:33-34`; R-fea).
2. **Unstable criterion on our data** (`research/resolute_eval/scores_summary.json`):

   | Dataset | PCA-BIC pick | UMAP-BIC pick | CH pick |
   |---|---|---|---|
   | P7513 MERSCOPE | 1.6 (K = 22) | 0.6 (K = 10) | 0.2 (K = 5) |
   | P1212 MERSCOPE | 1.8 (K = 16) | 0.4 (K = 6) | 0.3–0.4 (K = 4–6) |

   The pick moves 3–7 grid steps with the embedding (UMAP-BIC does land in the E6 plateau). Its reference comparisons used the old, wrong labels (best ARI vs "MMC broad" .77 on P7513; ≤ .235 on P1212), so this shows instability, not wrongness; the draft's "BIC always picks the top of the grid" was wrong (R-sci).
3. **Stability is not validity:** the stability rule passed 2/6 in E4 (replication 0.28), split P1212 Xenium Exc into 11 vs MERSCOPE's 3, and overshoots the corrected optimum by up to 4 steps (E6).
4. **Cost:** O(N²) stability, ~700 s at 40k cells (`timing2.log`), on 100–165k-cell sections.
5. **Kept:** the subsample-stability diagnostic idea, reimplemented in O(T·N) (§6.3). Its role is taken by map-first leaves plus the optional merge (§6.4); CHOIR and sc-SHC (R) are a v2 benchmark.

---

## 7. Problem 3: mouse multi-region annotation

### 7.1 Reference (WMB / Yao, CCN20230722): the validated configuration

- **Marker gene universe (M2 review, R28):** `cell_type_mapper`'s raw-CPM normalisation runs over the genes of the training h5ads, so the universe changes the markers. The validated lookups were built on the 899-gene ag7 ∪ VZG2 union (the `var` of `research/selfmap/query_{ag7,vzg2}_full.h5ad`), packaged as `wmb_marker_universe_ag7_vzg2.csv`; a panel inside it uses it and reproduces the validated lookups exactly (a per-panel universe differed in 301 / 142 of 368 parents on ag7 / VZG2). Any other panel uses its own genes and is recorded as not the validated configuration until M3's shadow run repeats MO1 / E3 on it. The 11,913 self-map test cells are a pinned input (sha256), required while resolvability is enabled and read from a stable copy in the store.
- **Markers** from a panel-restricted WMB precompute: ≤ 50 cells per cluster from the local **WMB-10Xv3** h5ads (`/media/mathieubo/SSD1/MerXen/mapmycells/abc_atlas/expression_matrices/WMB-10Xv3/20230630/`, all 13 region files: CB, CTXsp, HPF, HY, Isocortex-1/2, MB, MY, OLF, P, PAL, STR, TH), excluding the self-map test cells; the validated build has 240,529 cells covering 5,303 of 5,322 clusters (`research/selfmap/panel_ref/`, `build_marker_ref.py`, `.log`). **Correction to rev1**, which said the build also used "the 10Xv2 that exists locally": the validated build is 10Xv3 only, 10Xv3 covers every region, and no 10Xv2 or other download is needed for the coronal scope; four small hindbrain / midbrain subclasses exist only in 10X Multiome data and are a **documented limitation, not filled in** (user decision 2026-09-26; §7.8, OD-C3). The Allen stats file cannot supply markers (`n_cells` all 1, `sum` only; critic check 1).
- **Mapping means:** Allen `precomputed_stats_ABC_revision_230821.h5` (1.38 GB, all 32,285 genes, from `/srv/storage/Araceli-ageing/...`), **copied** with sha256 because it sits in another project's directory — exactly the pairing of every validated mouse result (critic check 6, E3, E5 timing, E7; R-sci, R-fea). Covering all genes, it serves any mouse panel, 5K included.
- **Settings:** `--drop_level CCN20230722_SUPT`, bf 0.5, 100 iterations, seed 0.
- **Self-built means are v2:** only after re-running E3, E7 and MO1–MO5 on ag7 and VZG2 original_seg with the new bundle, plus a self-map at N = 50 vs N = 100 (OD-C2); "cell-weighted centroids" does not hold (73% of leaves are capped at exactly 50 cells).
- **Gene IDs:** symbols map through WMB `gene.csv` (all 500 + 815 map); other panels via the resolver (§8.4); the 16 GB gene-mapper DB is not needed.
- **Evidence** (critic check 6, `research/selfmap/runs2`; ag7 unaffected by the VZG2 bug): ag7 quarter depth in silico class .841 → .959, subclass .784 → .911, Immune recall .39 → .95; real ag7 MY GABA 219 → 77 cells, CB GABA 150 → 22; ag7 marker consistency over all 88,931 marker-pseudo-confident cells .886 (bf 0.9 default) → .911 (panel lookup, bf 0.5), .963 at class bp ≥ 0.9 (`review_feasibility/mo1_ag7_marker_consistency.csv`).
- **VZG2:** only original_seg results are valid (`exp/E3/mmc/REAL_vzg2orig_panelLK.csv`, `exp/E5/mmc/rt_VZG2_original_seg_WMBpanelLK_np6.csv`); consistency .856 overall, .903 at class bp ≥ 0.9.

### 7.2 Region handling (E7 design; `annotation/mouse_regions.py`)

1. **Map unpruned**, kept as `mmc_wmb_unpruned_*`.
2. **Infer present regions** (`exp/E7/09_autoregion_v2.py`): confident neurons (class and subclass bp ≥ 0.9) → their subclass's MERFISH home division → each 150 µm tile with ≥ 3 such neurons gets its majority region → a region is present if it holds ≥ 1% of assigned tiles with a connected component of ≥ 3 tiles; OB is its own region; < 200 assigned tiles → skip pruning with a warning [L]. An explicit `mouse_section_regions` overrides inference; `none` disables pruning; M6b may add region coupling or never-drop classes or change the tile parameters (§7.8). Validation (E7 §4): inferred set = manual anatomy on ag7 and VZG2; on 53 MERFISH sections wrongly dropped cells average 0.029% (worst 0.72%; per AP bin in §7.8).
3. **Two-tier drop rule** (E7 §7): if < 20% of a class's MERFISH cells lie in present regions, drop its subclasses with present-region share < 0.3 (subclasses with < 20 MERFISH cells follow their class); otherwise drop subclasses with share < 0.1; never drop Astro-Epen, OPC-Oligo, Vascular, Immune, Pineal (plus any M6b addition); filter the lookup to the pruned tree (`reference.filter_lookup_to_tree`).
4. **Re-map only cells whose unpruned subclass was dropped** (`--nodes_to_drop`, `region_pruned_changed`); others keep their label by construction (E7's full re-map changed 0.05–0.25% of class labels outside dropped nodes, below seed noise 0.95–1.41%; subset vs full re-map compared in M6 [L]).
5. **QC-only columns:** F1 `flag_region_incoherent` (0.3–0.7% expected), `inferred_region`, class coherence vs intrinsic whole-brain coherence.

"T2 removed" is mechanical, not validation (pruning drops exactly the nodes that define T2; R-sci). Mouse validation is composition vs AP-matched MERFISH (MO4), marker consistency (MO1) and per-AP-bin harm (MO10). **Sagittal, OB- and CB-dominated sections are out of scope** (decision 4; sagittal needs another region model): use an explicit `mouse_section_regions` or `none`; the report warns when the AP estimate falls outside AP 2.4–10.4 mm.

### 7.3 Mouse confidence (v1; D-M4)

- **Class:** raw bp ≥ 0.9, counts ≥ 20, not region-dropped. **Subclass:** raw bp ≥ 0.8, counts ≥ 50. **Supertype:** never a leaf; report-only, opt-in (OD-E4, decided). For panels other than ag7 / VZG2 these are minimums that resolvability may raise (§8.3).
- Class bp saturates (median 1.0; 74–83% of cells ≥ 0.9), so it is a weak gate; most protection comes from the region rule, floors, resolvability and the gate.
- `avg_correlation` is recorded (`ct_class_corr`); the M6 shadow evaluates floors 0.4 and 0.5 against coverage and marker consistency by depth and pre-registers one (legacy configuration: VZG2 agreement .68 at 0.4–0.5 vs .85 at 0.5–0.6 [M]). It depends on marker number, so other panels take their floor from the resolvability simulation [L].
- Mouse data are deep (ag7 proseg_hybrid q5 82, median 431; VZG2 original_seg median 984–990); the grid extends to 2000.
- **v2 mouse calibration:** (bp, avg_correlation, margin) fitted on a contamination simulation with majority-component truth, neighbour-donor spill from a parameter in `build_hash`, ≥ 30–50k cells. The draft's simulator drew spill from the circular MG_loose own-fraction and labelled mixtures as the minority type, which would have *depressed* Immune confidence although real Immune-call precision is .98–.99 (E3; R-sci, R-rob).
- **Correction to the draft:** Immune *calls* are not over-trusted (precision .98–.99); thinning calibration over-trusts Immune *recall* and host-label purity — cells carrying microglial transcripts are called as their host type (E3 verdict 4: Immune recall .988 at 25% spill, .425 at 50%, .025 at 67%).

### 7.4 Microglial spill-over and small cells

- **Segmentation mixing limits whole-cell typing:** marker-defined microglia have median microglial own-fraction .085–.12 (ag7) and .17–.21 (VZG2 original_seg) — [M], because MG_loose (Cx3cr1 + Csf1r + C1qa) shares three of the flag's five ag7 genes.
- **Independent evidence:** on valid VZG2 original_seg, MMC-panel composition already matches MERFISH (Immune 1.7% vs 1.50–1.56%; Astro-Epen 19.0% vs 17.2–17.5%; `review_scientific/merfish_window_class_shares.txt`, sections .31–.33); ag7 proseg_hybrid Immune 1.1%. The draft's "under-calling with an 8–17% ceiling" was a marker-set artefact (R-sci).
- **`flag_microglial_spillover`** (report-only) fires on 8.6% of VZG2 cells vs 1.5% microglia in MERFISH, so it measures **spill-over prevalence**, never microglia counts. Held-out validation on VZG2: P2ry12, Tmem119, Siglech, C1qb, Trem2, Gpr34, Ctss, Ptprc, Selplg, Blnk at 83–94 vs 2.3 per 1,000 in flagged vs unflagged cells (35–40×); ag7 has only 5 microglial genes (no held-out test). Other panels derive the set automatically (§8.6).
- **Not adopted:** a full-profile flag (12–28% flagged, driven by immediate-early genes); an OPC flag (Olig1/2, Gpr17 shared with oligodendrocytes); relabelling by MIX or LKloc. LKloc is an optional report-only second opinion (`annotation_mouse_lkloc=false`; VZG2 OPC .68 → .87, Oligo .75 → .92, Astro .78 → .70).
- **Astro-Epen low-count sink:** 46% of cells under 100 counts are called Astro (~1.1% of cells); `flag_astro_lowcount` and Astro-Epen shown with and without them. **OPC composition** only after a pericyte-free OPC marker validation (ag7 OPC recall .31 against a partly pericyte set; E3).
- **Segmentation as the lever:** ag7 reseg is cleanest (MMC–marker .920 vs .886; doublet-like 8.8% vs 15.1%); E8 in M3; default segmentation OD-B7 (decided 2026-09-27: proseg_hybrid for human, reseg for mouse).

### 7.5 Segmentation and data validity

- **VZG2:** original_seg only until M0a is fixed and re-run; `research/selfmap/runs2/REAL_vzg2_*` and `research/vzg2_mmc/*` (invalid proseg_hybrid) are invalid.
- **Failed registration check** (M0a guard; MERSCOPE and Xenium, §8.6): annotation still runs; mouse gate `failed`; report banner; `exclude_hard` on every cell; with `qc_registration_strict=true` the QC stage fails instead.

### 7.6 Mouse dataset gate (`annotation/mouse_gate.py`; label-free signals)

| Signal | Source | Failed if | Warning if |
|---|---|---|---|
| G1 registration | M0a `segmentation_registration_check`: transcript density within 4 µm of centroids vs random points; shift against the platform's own segmentation (Vizgen cells / Xenium Onboard Analysis cells) | ratio < 1.5 (invalid VZG2 174 / 146 = 1.19; valid 421 / 146 ≈ 2.9) or shift > 5 µm | ratio < 2.0 |
| G2 marker referee | Class-level canonical-marker consistency over marker-pseudo-confident cells (per-panel marker sets, §8.6) | < 0.70 | < 0.80 |
| G3 implausibility | Pre-pruning region-implausible (T2) share | – | > 3% |
| G4 composition | vs the AP-matched MERFISH window (AP estimate, §7.8) | – | Astro-Epen outside ±5 points or Immune outside ±1 point (warning only: aged or lesioned tissue such as ag7 differs, OD-C4; provisional bands on Xenium [L]) |
| G5 spill-over | `flag_microglial_spillover` rate | – | > 15% |

Negative control: the invalid VZG2 proseg_hybrid must be `failed` (G1) with a G4 warning (Astro-Epen 34.3% vs 17.4%). Positive controls: VZG2 original_seg and ag7 proseg_hybrid not `failed` (level `full`, with or without the warning flag; the mouse gate uses the same level + warning-flag form as the human gate). Per-cell confidence alone cannot catch invalid data (class bp ≥ 0.9 covers .742 of invalid VZG2 vs .749 of ag7; R-rob).

### 7.7 Mouse vs human configuration

| Aspect | Human | Mouse |
|---|---|---|
| Primary | WHB frontal SUPC→CLUS, panel n30 | WMB panel lookup (10Xv3 precompute) + Allen means, drop SUPT |
| Second vote | SEA-AD MR (v1); LL (v1.1) | none; flags and gate instead |
| Levels | lineage, broad, nt, supercluster; SEA subclass reported; cluster report-only (OD-E4) | broad, class, nt, subclass; supertype report-only (OD-E4) |
| Branch : leaf | broad(+NT) : supercluster | class : subclass |
| Region | `anatomical_region` (frontal only validated; extensible) | auto inference + two-tier pruning, coronal AP 2.4–10.4 mm |
| Resolvability test cells | HO donor with HO bundle | ≤ 10 per supertype self-map cells (+ up to 30 per non-neuronal supertype) |
| Platform QC | aligned-bin M vs X; set c or intersection | MERFISH composition; G1 vs the platform's own segmentation |
| Gate | A-gate + trust state (+ v1.1 bundle-predicted) | G1–G5 + trust state |

### 7.8 Coronal AP-axis coverage (rev2; M6b)

**Scope (decision 4, 2026-09-26):** coronal sections along the whole AP axis except CB- or OB-dominated ones — e.g. anterior striatum / pallidum / septum, hypothalamic levels, hippocampal / thalamic levels like VZG2 and ag7, posterior midbrain / pons without cerebellum. Sagittal, CB and OB sections are out for now (R22).

**The references cover the scope.** WMB-10Xv3 has files for all 13 divisions and the validated precompute uses only 10Xv3 [H]. Of 5,322 WMB clusters, 10 have no 10Xv3 cell (`plan_rev2/ap_axis/uncovered_clusters.csv`) [H]: 9 hindbrain / cerebellar clusters sequenced only by 10X Multiome and 1 cortical L2/3 IT cluster (alias 238, 111 cells) sequenced only by 10Xv2 inside subclass 007, which has 17 other clusters. None is STR, PAL or OLF. Four whole subclasses lack any 10Xv3 cluster — `157 RN Spp1 Glut`, `279 PSV Pax2 Gly-Gaba`, `280 NLL-po Pax7 Gaba`, `297 CU-ECU Pax2 Gly-Gaba` (`uncovered_subclass_check.log`); the first three are in scope but small (≤ 0.2% of cells in any in-scope MERFISH section, AP 7.5–10.3; 0.023% overall) [H]. They are Multiome-only, so 10Xv2 would not help. **Decision (user, 2026-09-26): these are not filled in; they are a documented limitation.** The validated 10Xv3 build is kept unchanged, and no Multiome cells are added. Query cells of these types cannot receive these subclass labels: they map to the nearest covered subclass or stay `unresolved` at subclass level. The limitation is (i) listed in `docs/stages/annotation.md` ("Known limitations"), (ii) recorded in each mouse bundle's `bundle.json` (`uncovered_subclasses`, `uncovered_clusters`) and resolvability table, and (iii) shown in the mouse report with a note on sections at AP ≥ 7.5, where they can occur. **No download is needed.** (9 further clusters are absent because their only sampled 10Xv3 cells are self-map test cells, 5,312 − 5,303 [M]; production keeps that exclusion so the test set stays disjoint.)

**What E7 validated, per AP bin (rev2 re-analysis).** E7 ran the rule on 53 MERFISH-638850 sections with Allen labels thinned to density 0.42 ("confident neurons" = Allen `average_correlation_score ≥ 0.5`, not our bootstrap rule). Re-binned (`plan_rev2/ap_axis/ap_bin_summary.py`, `.log`, `sections_mf0.01_mc3.csv`; v1 rule: 150 µm tiles, ≥ 3 neurons, ≥ 1% of tiles, component ≥ 3 tiles); in scope = CB + OB < 50% of grey cells: 38 sections (AP 2.4–10.4 mm); out: 4 OB (AP < 2.3) and 11 CB (AP ≥ 10.6).

| AP bin (mm) | Content | In-scope sections | Sections missing a region | Wrongly dropped, mean / max | Sections > 0.1% |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2.4–3.0 | AON / OLF, OB edge | 3 | 0 | 0 / 0 | 0 |
| 3.0–4.5 | anterior OLF, STR, septum | 6 | 3 (OB; CTXsp + PAL ×2) | 0.13% / **0.72%** | 1 |
| 4.5–6.0 | STR, PAL, anterior HY | 7 | 3 (CTXsp) | 0.056% / 0.15% | 2 |
| 6.0–7.5 | TH, HY, caudal STR | 8 | 4 (CTXsp, HPF, PAL ×2) | 0.007% / 0.055% | 0 |
| 7.5–9.0 | HPF, TH (ag7, VZG2 at 8.0–8.5) | 6 | 1 (CTXsp) | 0.003% / 0.021% | 0 |
| 9.0–10.4 | MB, P (CB sliver ≤ 26%) | 8 | 0 (extra MY / OLF / P: less pruning, safe) | 0.009% / 0.029% | 0 |

- **The harm is anterior** (`plan_rev2/ap_axis/wrongly_dropped_nodes.csv`): at AP 3.34 (section .57) a missed OB sliver (1.9% of grey cells) drops the whole class `05 OB-IMN GABA` — 154 cells (0.7%), 143 in OLF (AON / olfactory peduncle), i.e. RMS neuroblasts and OB-type interneurons outside the bulb. At AP 4.4–5.3 OB-type interneuron subclasses found in STR and OLF (`039 OB Meis2 Thsd7b Gaba`, `041/042/043 OB-in/out/mi Frmd7 Gaba`, `044 OB Dopa-Gaba`) and `025 CA2-FC-IG Glut` (indusium griseum) are dropped at 0.01–0.07% each. Behind AP 6 at most ~26 cells (0.055%) per section are affected (section .44 at AP 6.05: 11 `04 DG-IMN Glut`, 6 `03 OB-CR Glut`, 6 `008 L2/3 IT ENT Glut`, 3 others). Missed CTXsp / PAL slivers cost ≤ 0.06%.
- **Measured directly, the picture holds, and the oracle metric is shown to be incomplete** (reviewer check, `plan_rev2/review_generality/{prod_drop,oracle_drop}.py`, `prod_indrop_sections.csv`): confident Allen cells (`average_correlation_score ≥ 0.6`) whose class or subclass is in the production drop list, mean / max per bin: 0.15% / 0.70% (AP 3.0–4.5; section .57, mostly OB-IMN), 0.08% / 0.11% (4.5–6.0), 0.04% / 0.07% (6.0–7.5), 0.05% / 0.10% (7.5–9.0), 0.007% / 0.03% (9.0–10.4). The E7 metric counts only drops absent under the CCF-truth regions, and CCF truth is itself incomplete: at section .56 (AP 3.52) the CCF division shares give STR = 0, so the oracle drop list would remove 2.65% of confident cells (462 `060 OT D3 Folh1 Gaba` plus STR D1 / D2 cells, parcellated as OLF); production keeps them only because inference adds an "extra" STR. M6b therefore scores harm directly and treats extra regions as neutral.
- **Global alternatives are worse:** `min_tile_fraction` 0.005 lowers the in-scope maximum to 0.082%, but VZG2 then calls P present from the 243 / MRN sink patches, losing the pruning that removes the main mouse sink (E7 §4); 0% misses nothing but loses about half of the pruning.
- **Rule parameters along the axis:** in-scope sections have 603–2,100 assigned tiles (minimum at AP 2.4), so the 200-tile guard never fires on full coronal sections [M]; hemisections (about half as many tiles) still pass; small crops skip pruning with a warning (fails safe). The 1% presence rule is right for posterior MB / P and fails only for OB slivers next to the AON. Rev2 varied only `min_tile_fraction` (0, 0.005, 0.01) and `min_component_tiles` (1, 3); the 150 µm tile and ≥ 3 neurons per tile were not re-examined, so M6b variant (f) scores them. The never-drop list is right along the axis but lacks the OB-lineage neuroblasts of the SVZ / RMS / AON. **AON is OLF** in the CCF parcellation (E7's OB = MOB, AOB, `In`, and OLF-unassigned at AP < 2.5), so AON cells are **not** excluded; the risk is OB-home types in AON sections when the OB sliver is missed. **Septum:** the lateral septal complex (LSr, LSc, LSv, SF, SH) is STR and the medial septum / diagonal band (MS, NDB, TRS) PAL in the CCF parcellation (MERFISH-638850 metadata; OT is STR too), so septal sections are covered by the STR and PAL homes [H].

**M6b design (pre-registered now).**
1. **Production-rule validation on MERFISH:** build the three WMB panel bundles (MERFISH 500, MERFISH ∩ ag7 188, MERFISH ∩ VZG2 346; ~1.5–2 h each at the measured rates) and map every in-scope MERFISH-638850 section (30k-cell subsample each, ~1.1M cells) with the production WMB configuration on those gene sets; by OD-E1 these mappings serve only the AP-axis rule and do not validate a new panel (the 500-gene panel card is reported as a diagnostic only). Needs the MERFISH-638850 expression h5ad (~7.6 GB from ABC; not on SSD1): **download approved 2026-09-26 (OD-C8)**, to `/srv/storage/MerXen/abc_atlas/MERFISH-C57BL6J-638850/` [L: path], by the operator, with sha256 recorded. **Out of sample:** region shares and subclass homes (`wmb_region_share`) are recomputed leaving out each section and its immediate neighbours (± 1 section). Run region inference and pruning with our bootstrap-based confident neurons; report per AP bin inferred vs CCF regions, missed and extra regions; **harm** = share of confident Allen-labelled cells (`average_correlation_score ≥ 0.6` and our class bp ≥ 0.9 on the MERFISH mapping) whose class or subclass is in the production drop list; relabelled cells; T2 after pruning; nodes dropped. The E7 oracle-relative harm (cells in nodes dropped under inferred but not under CCF-truth regions) is a diagnostic only; extra regions are neutral.
2. **Candidate rule changes**, scored on that table plus ag7 and VZG2 original_seg: (a) coupling `{"OB": ["OLF"]}` — OB counts as present when OLF is present and AON-home subclasses hold ≥ 1% of tiles, with AON-home defined at structure level (`parcellation_structure == "AON"`; the home model is division-level today); (b) never-drop `05 OB-IMN GABA` when STR or OLF is present; (c) keep `025 CA2-FC-IG Glut` when Isocortex and STR are present; (d) an OB-only `min_tile_fraction` 0.005; (e) *withdrawn* (user decision 2026-09-26: the uncovered posterior subclasses are a documented limitation, not filled in); (f) tile parameters: `tile_um` 100 / 200 and `min_neurons_per_tile` 2 (in-scope sections have 603–2,100 assigned 150 µm tiles). **Selection (pre-registered; OD-C8, decided 2026-09-26: the rule picks among (a)–(d) and (f)):** lowest maximum in-scope (direct) harm, subject to MO2 on ag7 and VZG2 and T2 ≤ 0.1% in every bin; ties → the simpler rule; recorded in the preregistration before MO10 is scored (OD-C8).
3. **First real anterior section** (STR / PAL / septum level), when it arrives: inferred regions vs manual anatomy, pruning mechanics, mouse gate, composition vs its AP-matched MERFISH window (MO11).
4. **AP estimate:** the composition-matching estimate prototyped in `data/mouse_*` (E7 §5 step 1) labels each section's AP bin and chooses the G4 window from all MERFISH sections.
5. **Optional second series:** Zhuang ABCA-1/2 (1,122-gene MERFISH, independent animals; `research/data/zhuang_abca1_gene.csv`) if the leave-neighbours-out result is borderline [L]; it needs a separate download approval (not covered by OD-C8).

---

## 8. Panel and platform generality (v1 requirement; rev2, with the rev3 decisions)

Coming soon: Xenium 5K panels, custom Xenium v1 panels of unknown composition, and custom MERSCOPE panels, for human and mouse (mouse on Xenium too). Human tissue stays frontal cortex. This section makes the pipeline safe on panels it has never seen. New panels are validated by simulation only (OD-E1 / OD-E9, decided 2026-09-26; §8.8).

### 8.1 Principles

- **Nothing about a panel is hard-coded:** gene count, markers, emitted levels, thresholds and floors come from its bundle. Today's hard-codes (`never_emit_levels`; `EXPECTED_PANEL_GENE_COUNT = 300` at `clustering_squidpy.py:47`, legacy provenance only) are not used by map_first.
- **Every automatic decision is recorded** (bundle, provenance, report) **and fails towards coarser labels** (broad-only or refused rather than wrong fine labels).
- **Trust is earned per panel family** through a reviewed PR: the seeded families on real data; new families by pre-registered **simulation** criteria (NP1–NP9, §14; OD-E1) with stricter margins that they keep. Automatic checks alone give at most `provisional`, and real-data QC can only downgrade (§8.8).
- **Deeper data resolve finer levels only where resolvability says so;** leaves stay at WHB supercluster / WMB subclass, finer levels are report-only and opt-in (OD-E4, decided 2026-09-26).
- **Panels and families:** a panel is its **declared** gene list — Xenium `gene_panel.json` or the source-table `var`, the MERSCOPE codebook — after control removal and ID resolution; `panel_hash` (sha256 of those sorted IDs) keys the bundle. Never the observed or filtered `var`: zero-detection probes, section crops and the `filter_genes(min_cells=5)` applied before the clustered H5AD is written (`clustering_squidpy.py:452`; the prepared H5AD is unfiltered) would give the same data different hashes in the shadow and pipeline paths and force 3–12 h rebuilds for 5K panels. At MAP the lookup is restricted to the genes the dataset has, with subset bundles only when markers are lost (§3.3). **Trust inheritance** (OD-E7): a panel inherits its family's trust state if it has the same species **and platform**, Jaccard ≥ 0.95 with the family and all of its root markers [L] — so a Xenium panel resembling VZG2 does not inherit MERSCOPE `validated`. (M3b: "same platform" means the family was validated on every platform the panel serves, so one platform of a pair family run `per_platform` keeps the family.) Example: human set a is 296 genes in all evidence and 297 after M0e (H2AX = Xenium H2AFX); both hashes are listed.

### 8.2 Panel diagnostics and trust states

**Diagnostics** (`panel_report.json` from ANNOTATE_PANEL; `bundle.json` from PREP; report item 1): gene-ID resolution (features in, controls removed by type, genes resolved by source, unresolved list, merged duplicates, species check); panel genes present per reference (`n_query_genes_used`); root-marker coverage and how many broad classes the root separates (root children with ≥ 10 markers); markers per parent (min / median) and weak parents (< 5 markers); auto-collapsed parents and the leaves they hide; per-type control counts and the per-cell control rate (QC); the resolvability summary (§8.3).

**Trust states** (per reference and panel, decided in PREP; thresholds pre-registered now [L], reviewed in M3b):

| State | Rule | Effect |
|---|---|---|
| `refused` | < 95% of non-control features resolve to IDs of the run's species; or the exact-case species test fails (`species_mismatch`, §8.4); or < 50 mapped genes; or > 5% of IDs carry the other species' prefix; or root < 10 markers; or broad fails the local emission rule (§8.3; target 0.90) at every grid depth ≤ 250 | MAP skipped for that reference. Primary: all table cells `not_attempted_gate`, gate `failed` (`panel_refused`), `exclude_hard`. SEA-AD: degraded mode `single_method` (§5.3). Report explains; no crash |
| `broad_only` | The leaf level is resolvable for fewer than half of the broad classes with ≥ 50 test cells, at every depth ≤ 250 | Leaves `unresolved` (`subcluster_status = not_resolvable_panel`); broad, lineage, NT as usual |
| `provisional` | Passes the above; family not in `validated_panels.csv` | Emission per resolvability with targets + 0.05 at ≥ 60 counts and + 0.10 below 60 counts, capped at 0.97 (supercluster / subclass 0.90 / 0.95; broad 0.95 / 0.97), because E2 measured 5–10 points of in-silico optimism below ~60 counts; mouse subclass floor ≥ 60 (mouse has no second vote); sets the gate **warning flag** and a report banner but never lowers the gate level; listed separately in pooled cross-dataset statistics |
| `validated` | Family in `assets/annotation/validated_panels.csv` with a `validation_basis`: `real_data` (the seeded families) or `simulation` (a gate-P PR with the simulation criteria NP1–NP9, OD-E1) | `real_data`: normal rules and targets up to `validated_max_level`; levels above it are treated as provisional. `simulation`: emission exactly as for a provisional panel (the §8.3 per-(level, class, depth) rule with the provisional margins, the mouse subclass floor and max-rule floors; simulation is optimistic, E2 verdict 5), so promotion never changes what is emitted. Validation is per (level, class) (`validated_panel_levels.csv`, §4.7): confident labels of a validated (level, class) at depths ≥ its `validated_min_depth` are marked `ct_<L>_validated`; a failed or unevaluable class stays provisional per class without blocking the others; `validated_max_level` is the family's headline level (§14). No provisional banner; the gate warning flag is set only when > 10% of a dataset's confident labels at an emitted level fall outside the validated region (`warn_unvalidated_share`) [L]; pooled in cohort statistics with the basis recorded. Either basis: real-data QC can downgrade a dataset (§8.8) |

Seeded validated families (`validation_basis = real_data`): human set a (296-gene evidence hash and post-M0e hash; the curated family set c, E5's 32 exclusions), ag7 (500), VZG2 (815). Resolvability still runs for them as a regression check (H18).

### 8.3 Resolvability self-map (`ANNOTATE_REFERENCE_PREP`; `annotation/resolvability.py`)

**Purpose:** decide, before any real cell is labelled, which levels this panel resolves, for which classes, at which depths, and with which thresholds and floors. It replaces `never_emit_levels` and is the non-calibrating core of the v1.1 simulation (§5.7).

**Method** (per primary reference and panel; cached in the bundle):
1. **Test cells.** Human: 20–30k cells of the HO donor (`annotation_calibration_holdout_donor = "auto"`: H19.30.002; gate P adds the two other frontal donors, §8.8), stratified by supercluster (≤ 1,000 each; all cells of rare ones), mapped against `whb_frontal_supc_clus_ho` built without that donor (`exp/E2/ho/` recipe). *User decision 2026-09-27 (H18 follow-up):* every non-neuronal supercluster is topped up to the same cap with WHB non-neuronal nuclei of E2's 14 neocortical dissections outside the frontal ROIs (`research/insilico/01b_extract_nonneurons.py`: MTG, STG, M1C, A43, A40, V1C, V2, A19, S1C, A1C, A5-A7, ITG, A38, A13), disjoint from every frontal reference, training and marker cell, recorded in the bundle (dissections, counts per class and donor), because the HO donor holds only 23 Vascular, 5 Fibroblast and 20 COP cells on set a. *User decision 2026-09-27 (implemented 2026-09-28, `HO_OTHER_REGION_VERSION` 2):* only cells of clusters the HO training reference keeps (≥ 5 training cells) are drawn; the candidates of absent clusters are left out and counted in the bundle (the first draw held 694 such cells, whose cluster truth no call can name). SEA-AD's 7-class precision is measured on the same cells via the vocab, so SEA thresholds can be checked too. Mouse: the 11,913 self-map test cells (≤ 10 per supertype; `research/selfmap/truth.csv`) plus up to 30 per non-neuronal supertype from cells the marker build did not use, all excluded from marker building; they sit inside the Allen means (~12k of 4M cells; negligible leakage, mouse report §4.1).
2. **Panel and depth:** restrict to the panel genes the reference contains; thin binomially to each grid value D (human ≤ 1,000 genes `[10,15,30,60,120,250]`; mouse and > 1,000 genes `[10,20,50,100,250,500,1000,2000]`) **only the cells whose native panel counts are ≥ D**, recording n per (class, D). Thinning cannot raise counts, and reference cells often cannot reach the top bins: of the WMB test cells on the ag7 / VZG2 panels only 66% / 75% reach 1,000 counts (19% / 29% reach 2,000); class medians are Immune 96 / 139 (0 of 80 at ≥ 1,000), Vascular 220 / 336, Astro-Epen 320 / 527, OPC-Oligo 750 / 859 (reviewer check on `research/selfmap/query_{ag7,vzg2}_full.h5ad` + `truth.csv`, re-verified). **D_max(c)** = the deepest grid value with ≥ 50 test cells of class c.
3. **Recipe `R1_contam_HO`** (E2): per-gene efficiency LogNormal(0, 0.8) and 25% foreign-broad-class spill, truth = host type; a "clean" thinning-only run is reported as the upper bound; seed 0; recipe version in `build_hash`. *M3b final follow-up (2026-09-28, resolvability version 5):* every per-cell draw is keyed by what it simulates — a simulated cell's thinning and spill thinning by a stable hash of the seed, the recipe name and version, the cell id and D; its spill partner by rendezvous hashing (uniform over the eligible cells) — so changing the test set no longer redraws the other cells (one stream over all cells did; the review-2 rebuild moved 0–7 of 85 validated bins per human dataset). The gene efficiency is the pre-registered draw (one generator seeded by `[seed, 0x6566]` over the panel's gene order), which never depended on the test cells. *M3b review 3 (2026-09-28, resolvability version 6):* version 5 had also keyed each gene's efficiency by the gene id, which neither change needed and which replaced the pre-registered seed-0 realisation; version 6 restores it and keeps the per-cell keys.
4. **Map** with the bundle's production configuration (same markers, bf 0.5, 100 iterations).
5. **Tabulate** `resolvability.parquet`: per (level, parent class, D) n, precision and coverage at the default threshold, the precision-coverage curve over thresholds 0.50–0.99, the isotonic fit, per-node F1, within-parent confusion; plus `resolvability_cells.parquet` (per simulated cell: level, parent, D, truth, call, bp) for reweighting in RESOLVE.

**Decisions** (`resolvability_summary.json`; applied per cell in RESOLVE):
- **Local threshold rule (E2 verdict 3; moved from M10 into v1):** per (level L, parent class c, depth bin d), fit an isotonic map g(bp) of correctness on the simulated cells; t* = min{t ≥ default_L : g(t) ≥ target_L}, capped at 0.99. The set-level rule ("lowest threshold at which the accepted set reaches the target") is **not** used: E2 found it misses the target in 37–50% of dataset × depth combinations while the local rule met both targets in 100% (`exp/E2/out/thresholds2.csv`), and on large panels bp saturates, so the set above a threshold can look precise while cells near it are not. t* is chosen on one half of the test cells and checked on the other.
- **Emission:** L is emitted for (c, d) if t* exists, there are ≥ 50 confident calls, the Wilson 95% lower bound of their precision is ≥ target_L − 0.02 and coverage is ≥ 0.2 (targets + the provisional margins while provisional and for families validated by simulation, §8.2). *User decision 2026-09-27 (H18 follow-up), replacing the D_max inheritance:* the point precision must also reach target_L (one rule with the gate-P evaluation rules, §14); a bin with fewer than 50 confident calls takes the verdict of the deep-end pool — from the deep end, bins are pooled into a "≥ d" set, each test cell counted once at its deepest bin, until the set holds 50 confident calls (its shallowest bin is D_P) — judged by the same rule (point estimate, Wilson bound on the Kish n, t* fitted on the pool's fit half, the target of D_P); the verdict is carried to every pooled bin, whose cells are flagged `resolvability_extrapolated` (precision assumed not to fall with depth [M]); D_P keeps its own verdict when it holds 50 calls itself and is withdrawn when the pool fails; a class never reaching 50 confident calls even fully pooled is `not_resolvable` (`insufficient_calls`). *M3b review 2 (resolvability version 4):* every bin at or above D_P that holds 50 confident calls itself keeps its own verdict (withdrawn when the pool fails), as gate P tests it on its own; only bins not judged on their own take the pool's verdict, and only those deeper than D_P are flagged extrapolated; in RESOLVE only calls with a positive weight count towards the 50. The extrapolated share per class is on the panel card. If no bin of c has ≥ 50 test cells (D_max), L is not emitted for c (no pooling across classes, which neurons would dominate). Non-emitted → `not_resolvable`.
- **Composition reweighting (RESOLVE):** PREP's unweighted tables set only the panel trust state. RESOLVE refits g and the emission test per (L, c, d) with the simulated cells reweighted to the dataset's soft composition within c (§5.5), so emission and thresholds follow the dataset's own mix of types (E2 / R-sci: HO supercluster real-vs-sim agreement .44 unweighted vs .78 reweighted). Minutes per dataset. *M3b implementation (review, resolvability version 2):* the target composition is the dataset's soft composition per depth bin (its overall composition for bins with < 50 cells); a type with < 20 test cells in a bin takes its broad class's weight; weights are trimmed at 10× the bin median and renormalised; each decision records the Kish n and the largest single-cell weight share. *M3b review 2 (resolvability version 4):* the trim is applied within each tested set (a (L, c, d) bin's calls or a pooled set) at 10× the set's median positive weight: the bin median is set by its commonest test types, so every deep glial type hit the same cap and the reweighting did nothing inside a called glial class. The validated regime checks the pre-registered default on both halves (it is fitted on no test cell); t* is still fitted on one half and checked on the other. The WHB self-map applies the §5.2 COP rule to its broad calls.
- **Thresholds:** for panels without real-data validation (provisional, or validated by simulation) the per-(L, c, d) t*, never below the validated default (`threshold_source = resolvability_local`). Panels validated on real data keep their pre-registered thresholds (E2 derived 0.73 / 0.69 with the same local rule); disagreements are reported (H18).
- **Floors:** panels without real-data validation use max(known floor, `derive_floors` on the simulation) (§5.4). **Mouse `avg_correlation` floor:** for panels without real-data validation, the smallest value keeping class precision ≥ target at the median depth [L].
- **Fine levels (OD-E4, decided 2026-09-26: report-only, opt-in):** WHB cluster / WMB supertype only if `annotation_allow_fine_levels = true`, the emission rule passes, and a seed-1 re-simulation changes ≤ 2% of confident labels (for scale: the seed alone changes 15.5% of SEA-AD supertype labels at bf 0.5, E5, and 1.6–3.0% of WMB subclass labels, E7). Reported as `ct_cluster_*` / `ct_supertype_*`; never in `ct_final`, `ct_leaf` or MENDER keys in v1.
- **Dataset summary:** RESOLVE reports each level's "resolvable share" of table cells (e.g. "supercluster resolvable for 64% of cells").

**Expected on validated panels (H18):** human set a — broad and supercluster emitted for classes with n ≥ 50 from about their E2 floors; 0.73 / 0.69 meet targets (H6, now computed in PREP); WHB cluster not emitted (calibrated coverage ≤ 5.7%, E2; sibling clusters share a median of 9 panel binary DEGs). Mouse ag7 / VZG2 — class and subclass emitted at their real depths (self-map subclass .952 / .971 at matched depth, clean recipe; mouse report §4.2); supertype not emitted [L] (accuracy .79 in the bp 0.8–0.95 bin at bf 0.5).

**Cost** (≤ 815 genes): human ~35 min per panel incl. extraction (18 min) and HO markers, of which ~11 min mapping (25k cells × 6 depths × 2 recipes at 13.4 processor-s per 1k) [M]; mouse ~25 min (11,913 × 8 × 2 at 41.7 processor-s per 1k on 6 processes; fewer cells at deep bins) [M]; isotonic fits and reweighting take minutes. 5K: §8.7.

### 8.4 Gene IDs and control features (`annotation/panel.py`, `annotation/gene_ids.py`)

**Controls** (one registry, `assets/annotation/control_features.yaml`):
1. Feature types come from the Xenium **source** table (spatialdata-io keeps `feature_types`), the transcripts' `codeword_category` or `gene_panel.json`, carried to the ProSeg-derived tables through the same lookup path as gene IDs (`collect_gene_id_lookup_for_samples`), because the enriched tables MerXen annotates hold symbols (plus looked-up IDs) and no feature types (`clustering_squidpy.py:2753-2754`). Keep only `Gene Expression`; every other type — negative-control probe / codeword, unassigned, deprecated, genomic control, intergenic [L: exact strings taken from the 10x and Vizgen documentation of control probe / codeword names in M3b, before the first 5K or custom dataset is segmented, and confirmed on that dataset's `codeword_category` / feature types, OD-D12; no public 5K output is downloaded, OD-E1] — is a control, with per-cell counts kept as QC.
2. Otherwise anchored name patterns: MERSCOPE `^Blank-\d+$`; Xenium `^(BLANK|NegControl(Probe|Codeword)|UnassignedCodeword|DeprecatedCodeword|GenomicControl|Intergenic)[_-]`.
3. Last resort: the existing `CONTROL_TOKENS` substring rule; a feature resolving to a reference gene is never removed by a substring, and an anchored match is never kept.
4. On current panels the registry removes exactly what `remove_control_features` removes (test). `CONTROL_TOKENS` lacks "intergenic". The ProSeg transcript filter (`segmentation/pipeline.py:527`) excludes `^(Deprecated|NegControl|Unassigned|Intergenic)` on Xenium only — no `BLANK` or `GenomicControl` — and nothing on MERSCOPE [H code; 5K impact L]; a `[bugfix]` to `main` extends it from the 10x / Vizgen documentation (checked in M3b) **before** the first 5K or custom dataset is segmented, because a check on that dataset could not protect it; that dataset's `codeword_category` / feature types then confirm the filter, and a control name it misses triggers a follow-up fix and re-segmentation of the affected datasets; after the first species gate the filter is driven from the registry (OD-D12).
